# QSP Disease Model Library

> A **living, open model library** in which **Claude Code Routine (CCR)** picks one
> disease every day, builds a **Quantitative Systems Pharmacology (QSP)** model for
> it from start to finish, and commits the result directly to `main`.

![models](https://img.shields.io/badge/models-416-blue) ![framework](https://img.shields.io/badge/QSP-mrgsolve%20%C2%B7%20Shiny%20%C2%B7%20Graphviz-success) ![automation](https://img.shields.io/badge/built%20by-Claude%20Code%20Routine-orange)

The library currently holds finished QSP models for **416 diseases**. Each model
consists of four deliverables: (1) a mechanistic map, (2) an mrgsolve ODE model,
(3) a Shiny dashboard, and (4) a reference list. The full list is in the
[Model Gallery](#-model-gallery) below.

> **Note on this file.** This is the English translation of the upstream
> [`README.md`](../../README.md), maintained in this fork. The gallery table below is
> generated by `translations/tools/build_root_readme.py`; see
> [`translations/README.md`](../README.md) for why translations live in their own
> tree rather than as edits to the originals.

---

## 1. Project Overview

This repository is not a static collection of models designed by a human in one
sitting. It is **a library that grows as an automated AI agent adds one new disease
model every day**. In each session, Claude Code:

1. Picks a disease not yet covered.
2. Draws a **mechanistic map of the disease pathophysiology**, based on the current
   literature and clinical trial data.
3. Writes an **mrgsolve-based ODE model** linking drug PK/PD to disease progression.
4. Builds a **Shiny dashboard** for exploring and comparing parameters.
5. Compiles the **references** underpinning every assumption and parameter.
6. Commits and pushes the deliverables.

The goal is a **reference collection of reproducible, transparent, and
educationally usable QSP models** spanning a wide range of therapeutic areas.

## 2. What is QSP?

**Quantitative Systems Pharmacology (QSP)** combines systems biology with
pharmacokinetics/pharmacodynamics (PK/PD) to express the causal chain running from
**drug to target to pathway to disease to patient** mathematically, usually as
ordinary differential equations (ODEs). It goes beyond a purely statistical
dose-response curve and describes, at the level of mechanism, **why and how**
efficacy and toxicity arise.

A QSP model sets out to answer questions such as:

- If this target is modulated, how do the downstream pathways and the clinical
  biomarkers move?
- Combining drugs with different mechanisms of action, what synergy or antagonism
  appears?
- In which patient subtypes (genotype, severity, comorbidity) does the response
  differ?
- Which dose and dosing interval optimise the balance between efficacy and safety?

## 3. Why QSP Matters in Drug Development

QSP has become a central tool of the **Model-Informed Drug Development (MIDD)**
paradigm that regulators encourage. It contributes across the whole development
cycle:

| Stage | Contribution of QSP |
|------|------------|
| **Target discovery and validation** | Pathway-level simulation to assess in advance how modulating a target affects the disease phenotype, and to predict bypass pathways and resistance mechanisms |
| **Elucidating mechanism of action (MoA)** | Integrating in vitro / in vivo data to quantify the causal structure of the drug effect and to establish a biomarker strategy |
| **Dose and regimen selection** | Mechanism-based prediction of the first-in-human dose, the appropriate regimen, and the therapeutic window, putting phase 1 design on a rational footing |
| **Clinical trial design** | Simulating the response of patient subpopulations to optimise patient selection, stratification, endpoints, and sample size |
| **Translation** | Quantifying species and population differences by extrapolating animal to human, adult to paediatric, and healthy subject to patient |
| **Combination strategy** | Exploring the combined effect (synergy / antagonism) of different targets in virtual patient cohorts |
| **Regulatory communication** | Serving as an evidence package within the FDA and EMA MIDD frameworks, supporting trial waivers and label extensions |

A large share of drug development failures comes from **insufficient efficacy** and
**unanticipated safety problems**. QSP tests hypotheses *in silico* before a
candidate enters the clinic, which **reduces late-stage attrition** and helps stop
programmes that are going to fail sooner and more cheaply (fail fast). This library
provides **reproducible worked examples for teaching and research**, applying that
QSP workflow across a wide range of diseases.

## 4. Four Deliverables per Model

Every disease directory follows the same four-deliverable scheme.

| Deliverable | File | Description |
|--------|------|------|
| 🗺️ **Mechanistic map** | `*_qsp_model.dot` / `.svg` / `.png` | Pathophysiology and drug-action network drawn with Graphviz (100+ nodes, 8+ clusters) |
| ⚙️ **mrgsolve model** | `*_mrgsolve_model.R` | ODE model linking drug PK to disease PD (15+ compartments, 5+ treatment scenarios, notes on calibration against clinical trials) |
| 📊 **Shiny dashboard** | `*_shiny_app.R` | Interactive app for exploring patient profile, PK, PD, clinical endpoints, scenario comparison, and biomarkers (6+ tabs) |
| 📚 **References** | `*_references.md` | 30+ PubMed citations, grouped by section |

Each directory also contains **its own `README.md`** summarising these
deliverables, so the disease overview, key pathways, model specification, and
instructions for running it can be taken in at a glance.

## 5. How It Is Built

- **Automated generation**: Claude Code Routine picks one disease per day, writes the
  four deliverables plus the directory README, then commits and pushes.
- **Duplicate avoidance**: diseases and directories that already exist are skipped, and
  categories are selected in rotation.
- **Quality standards**: the minimum bar is a mechanistic map (100+ nodes / 8+
  clusters), mrgsolve (15+ compartments / 5+ scenarios), Shiny (6+ tabs), and
  references (30+ papers).
- **Naming conventions**: directories are lower-case hyphenated English names; files
  follow `<abbr>_qsp_model.*`, `<abbr>_mrgsolve_model.R`, `<abbr>_shiny_app.R`, and
  `<abbr>_references.md`.

## 6. Technology Stack

| Tool | Purpose |
|------|------|
| **Graphviz** (`dot`) | Rendering the mechanistic map (`.dot` to `.svg`/`.png`) |
| **mrgsolve** (R) | ODE-based PK/PD/QSP simulation |
| **Shiny** (R) | Interactive simulation dashboard |
| **Claude Code Routine** | Daily automated model generation, documentation, and commit |

## 7. Usage

```bash
# 1) Render the mechanistic map (requires Graphviz)
dot -Tsvg <disease>/<abbr>_qsp_model.dot -o <abbr>_qsp_model.svg
dot -Tpng -Gdpi=150 <disease>/<abbr>_qsp_model.dot -o <abbr>_qsp_model.png
```

```r
# 2) Run the mrgsolve model (R)
install.packages(c("mrgsolve", "dplyr", "ggplot2"))
library(mrgsolve)
mod <- mread("<disease>/<abbr>_mrgsolve_model.R")
out <- mrgsim(mod, end = 365)
plot(out)

# 3) Run the Shiny dashboard
install.packages("shiny")
shiny::runApp("<disease>/<abbr>_shiny_app.R")
```

## 8. Repository Layout

```
qsp/
├── README.md                     # this document (the full model gallery)
├── CLAUDE.md                     # library operating and generation guidelines
├── <disease>/                    # one directory per disease (416 in total)
│   ├── README.md                 # per-disease summary document
│   ├── <abbr>_qsp_model.dot      # mechanistic map source
│   ├── <abbr>_qsp_model.svg/.png # rendered images
│   ├── <abbr>_mrgsolve_model.R   # mrgsolve ODE model
│   ├── <abbr>_shiny_app.R        # Shiny dashboard
│   └── <abbr>_references.md      # references
└── ...
```

Glucocorticoid replacement and crinecerfont (CRF1 antagonism).<br>[🗺️ Map](../../congenital-adrenal-hyperplasia/cah_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../congenital-adrenal-hyperplasia/cah_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../congenital-adrenal-hyperplasia/cah_references.md) · [📄 README](../../congenital-adrenal-hyperplasia/README.md) |

S.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../hidradenitis-suppurativa/hs_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../hidradenitis-suppurativa/hs_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../hidradenitis-suppurativa/hs_references.md) · [📄 README](../../hidradenitis-suppurativa/README.md) |
| 200 | Respiratory | [**High-Altitude Illness**<br><sub>HAI</sub>](../../high-altitude-illness/) | <a href="../../high-altitude-illness/hai_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../high-altitude-illness/hai_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="hai"></a> | Writing a single inspired oxygen tension as acting through three defences with different time constants (ventilation, hypoxic pulmonary vasoconstriction, and cerebral vasodilatation) makes the window of altitude illness exactly the period during which the two fast defences run unchecked until the slow one arrives.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../high-altitude-illness/hai_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../high-altitude-illness/hai_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../high-altitude-illness/hai_references.md) · [📄 README](../../high-altitude-illness/README.md) |

Combination antiretroviral therapy (ART).<br>[🗺️ Map](../../hiv-aids/hiv_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../hiv-aids/hiv_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../hiv-aids/hiv_references.md) · [📄 README](../../hiv-aids/README.md) |
| 202 | Oncology | [**Hodgkin Lymphoma**<br><sub>cHL</sub>](../../hodgkin-lymphoma/) | <a href="../../hodgkin-lymphoma/hl_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../hodgkin-lymphoma/hl_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="hl"></a> | Hodgkin/Reed-Sternberg (HRS, CD30+ CD15+ PAX5-dim) cells arising from germinal centre B cells survive on NF-kB (REL amplification, TNFAIP3/A20 loss), JAK/STAT (JAK2 9p24.1 amplification, SOCS1/PTPN1 loss, STAT3/5/6), PI3K/AKT/mTOR, NOTCH1, and AP-1/JUNB signalling.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../hodgkin-lymphoma/hl_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../hodgkin-lymphoma/hl_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../hodgkin-lymphoma/hl_references.md) · [📄 README](../../hodgkin-lymphoma/README.md) |
| 203 | Neurology | [**Huntington's Disease**<br><sub>HD</sub>](../../huntingtons-disease/) | <a href="../../huntingtons-disease/hd_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../huntingtons-disease/hd_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="hd"></a> | CAG repeat expansion (36 or more) produces and aggregates mHTT, degenerating striatal medium spiny neurons (MSN).<br>[🗺️ Map](../../huntingtons-disease/hd_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../huntingtons-disease/hd_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../huntingtons-disease/hd_references.md) · [📄 README](../../huntingtons-disease/README.md) |
| 204 | Oncology | [**Hypercalcaemia of Malignancy**<br><sub>MAH</sub>](../../hypercalcemia-of-malignancy/) | <a href="../../hypercalcemia-of-malignancy/mah_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../hypercalcemia-of-malignancy/mah_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="mah"></a> | Writing plasma calcium not as a stored quantity but as the residual of three arms — bone, gut, and kidney — shows that in the closed loop where hypercalcaemia itself pulls down the renal excretory ceiling, crisis arises from a saddle-node bifurcation rather than from a large number.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../hypercalcemia-of-malignancy/mah_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../hypercalcemia-of-malignancy/mah_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../hypercalcemia-of-malignancy/mah_references.md) · [📄 README](../../hypercalcemia-of-malignancy/README.md) |

| 206 | Haematology | [**Hypereosinophilic Syndrome**<br><sub>HES</sub>](../../hypereosinophilic-syndrome/) | <a href="../../hypereosinophilic-syndrome/hes_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../hypereosinophilic-syndrome/hes_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="hes"></a> | A heterogeneous group of disorders defined by persistent peripheral eosinophilia (1500/uL or more on two occasions at least a month apart) with eosinophil-mediated organ damage; in about 10-20% a cryptic del(4)(q12q12) creates the FIP1L1-PDGFRA fusion gene, whose constitutively active tyrosine kinase drives clonal proliferation through STAT5, PI3K-AKT, and RAS-MAPK…<br>[🗺️ Map](../../hypereosinophilic-syndrome/hes_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../hypereosinophilic-syndrome/hes_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../hypereosinophilic-syndrome/hes_references.md) · [📄 README](../../hypereosinophilic-syndrome/README.md) |

| 222 | Neurology | [**Spontaneous Intracerebral Haemorrhage**<br><sub>ICH</sub>](../../intracerebral-hemorrhage/) | <a href="../../intracerebral-hemorrhage/ich_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../intracerebral-hemorrhage/ich_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="ich"></a> | A 60-ODE model that writes the bleeding flux as the product of driving pressure, haemostatic incompetence, and the number of open bleeding points so that haematoma volume becomes an integral rather than a state, and separates the mass-effect channel from the haem-iron channel so that the U-shaped optimum blood pressure emerges from MAP appearing twice with opposite signs.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../intracerebral-hemorrhage/ich_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../intracerebral-hemorrhage/ich_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../intracerebral-hemorrhage/ich_references.md) · [📄 README](../../intracerebral-hemorrhage/README.md) |
| 223 | Gynaecology & Reproductive | [**Intrahepatic Cholestasis of Pregnancy**<br><sub>ICP</sub>](../../intrahepatic-cholestasis-of-pregnancy/) | <a href="../../intrahepatic-cholestasis-of-pregnancy/icp_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../intrahepatic-cholestasis-of-pregnancy/icp_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="icp"></a> | Splitting bile acids into five species and letting them cross the placenta in both directions reveals, through the fitted exponent, that the steepness of the 100 umol/L stillbirth threshold lies not in placental transport but in the threshold character of fetal myocardial connexin-43 uncoupling — and puts the drug that lowers total bile acids on a different axis from the drug that abolishes itch.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../intrahepatic-cholestasis-of-pregnancy/icp_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../intrahepatic-cholestasis-of-pregnancy/icp_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../intrahepatic-cholestasis-of-pregnancy/icp_references.md) · [📄 README](../../intrahepatic-cholestasis-of-pregnancy/README.md) |

| 225 | Haematology | [**Iron Deficiency Anaemia**<br><sub>IDA</sub>](../../iron-deficiency-anemia/) | <a href="../../iron-deficiency-anemia/ida_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../iron-deficiency-anemia/ida_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="ida"></a> | Writing oral iron absorption as the product of luminal available iron x DMT1 capacity x enterocyte export capacity derives the structure in which the hepcidin produced by the previous dose closes the gate on the next one, so fractional absorption and total absorbed amount order in opposite directions with dosing interval.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../iron-deficiency-anemia/ida_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../iron-deficiency-anemia/ida_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../iron-deficiency-anemia/ida_references.md) · [📄 README](../../iron-deficiency-anemia/README.md) |

| 228 | Autoimmune & Rheumatologic | [**Juvenile Idiopathic Arthritis**<br><sub>JIA</sub>](../../juvenile-idiopathic-arthritis/) | <a href="../../juvenile-idiopathic-arthritis/jia_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../juvenile-idiopathic-arthritis/jia_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="jia"></a> | Synovial inflammation and cartilage and bone destruction mediated by T cells, B cells, and cytokines (TNF-alpha, IL-6, IL-1beta, IL-18). Models seven subtypes: oligoarticular, polyarticular RF+/-, systemic (sJIA), ERA, and psoriatic. For sJIA, includes the NLRP3 to IL-1beta/IL-18 autoinflammatory loop and the macrophage activation syndrome (MAS) pathway.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../juvenile-idiopathic-arthritis/jia_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../juvenile-idiopathic-arthritis/jia_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../juvenile-idiopathic-arthritis/jia_references.md) · [📄 README](../../juvenile-idiopathic-arthritis/README.md) |
| 229 | Vasculitis | [**Kawasaki Disease**<br><sub>KD</sub>](../../kawasaki-disease/) | <a href="../../kawasaki-disease/kd_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../kawasaki-disease/kd_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="kd"></a> | An unknown trigger activates TLR/NLR innate immunity, then the NLRP3 inflammasome (caspase-1 and IL-1beta maturation), then a cytokine storm (IL-1beta, IL-6, TNF-alpha), then vascular endothelial activation (raised VCAM-1, ICAM-1, TF), destroying the coronary media and producing aneurysms (AHA Z-score classification: small z of 2.5 or more, medium 5 or more, giant 10 or more)…<br>[🗺️ Map](../../kawasaki-disease/kd_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../kawasaki-disease/kd_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../kawasaki-disease/kd_references.md) · [📄 README](../../kawasaki-disease/README.md) |
| 230 | Renal & Urologic | [**Kidney Transplant Rejection**<br><sub>KTX</sub>](../../kidney-transplant-rejection/) | <a href="../../kidney-transplant-rejection/ktx_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../kidney-transplant-rejection/ktx_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="ktx"></a> | Alloantigen recognition drives T-cell-mediated and donor-specific-antibody-mediated rejection, and suppressing it increases BK and CMV infection — the U-shaped trade-off of net immunosuppression.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../kidney-transplant-rejection/ktx_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../kidney-transplant-rejection/ktx_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../kidney-transplant-rejection/ktx_references.md) · [📄 README](../../kidney-transplant-rejection/README.md) |

Paraneoplastic with SCLC in 50-60%. Six scenarios: amifampridine (3,4-DAP K+ blockade, Emax with EC50 120 ng/mL), prednisolone, plasma exchange, IVIG, and chemotherapy.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../lambert-eaton-syndrome/lems_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../lambert-eaton-syndrome/lems_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../lambert-eaton-syndrome/lems_references.md) · [📄 README](../../lambert-eaton-syndrome/README.md) |

Steroids and immunosuppression.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../mixed-connective-tissue-disease/mctd_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../mixed-connective-tissue-disease/mctd_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../mixed-connective-tissue-disease/mctd_references.md) · [📄 README](../../mixed-connective-tissue-disease/README.md) |
| 260 | Neurology | [**MOG Antibody-Associated Disease**<br><sub>MOGAD</sub>](../../mog-antibody-associated-disease/) | <a href="../../mog-antibody-associated-disease/mogad_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../mog-antibody-associated-disease/mogad_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="mogad"></a> | MOG antibody binds the outermost myelin layer and causes complement- and phagocyte-mediated demyelination while oligodendrocytes survive, so remyelination is possible, and relapse risk depends not on antibody titre but on where in the pathway the drug acts.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../mog-antibody-associated-disease/mogad_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../mog-antibody-associated-disease/mogad_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../mog-antibody-associated-disease/mogad_references.md) · [📄 README](../../mog-antibody-associated-disease/README.md) |
| 261 | Neurology | [**Moyamoya Disease**<br><sub>MMD</sub>](../../moyamoya-disease/) | <a href="../../moyamoya-disease/mmd_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../moyamoya-disease/mmd_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="mmd"></a> | Taking arteriolar dilatory reserve rather than cerebral blood flow as the state variable shows that at a critical inflow conductance the loss of reserve, pressure-passivity, and the sign reversal of the acetazolamide response all occur at the same point, and derives the infarction threshold from oxygen arithmetic.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../moyamoya-disease/mmd_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../moyamoya-disease/mmd_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../moyamoya-disease/mmd_references.md) · [📄 README](../../moyamoya-disease/README.md) |

Proteasome inhibitors, IMiDs, and anti-CD38.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../multiple-myeloma/mm_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../multiple-myeloma/mm_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../multiple-myeloma/mm_references.md) · [📄 README](../../multiple-myeloma/README.md) |

Disease-modifying therapy (anti-CD20, S1P modulators).<br>[🗺️ Map](../../multiple-sclerosis/ms_qsp.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../multiple-sclerosis/ms_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../multiple-sclerosis/ms_references.md) · [📄 README](../../multiple-sclerosis/README.md) |
| 265 | Neurology | [**Multiple System Atrophy**<br><sub>MSA</sub>](../../multiple-system-atrophy/) | <a href="../../multiple-system-atrophy/msa_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../multiple-system-atrophy/msa_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="msa"></a> | A 61-ODE model that writes standing blood pressure as the product of baroreceptor input, central integration, preganglionic output, postganglionic NE stores, alpha1 receptor density, and circulating volume, from which it follows which pressor works depending on whether the lesion is central or postganglionic.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../multiple-system-atrophy/msa_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../multiple-system-atrophy/msa_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../multiple-system-atrophy/msa_references.md) · [📄 README](../../multiple-system-atrophy/README.md) |

Cholinesterase inhibition and FcRn/complement inhibitors.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../myasthenia-gravis/mg_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../myasthenia-gravis/mg_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../myasthenia-gravis/mg_references.md) · [📄 README](../../myasthenia-gravis/README.md) |
| 267 | Oncology | [**Myelodysplastic Syndrome**<br><sub>MDS</sub>](../../myelodysplastic-syndrome/) | <a href="../../myelodysplastic-syndrome/mds_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../myelodysplastic-syndrome/mds_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="mds"></a> | CHIP to MDS clonal evolution; SF3B1/SRSF2/U2AF1/ZRSR2 splicing mutations (ring sideroblast formation, ABCB7 loss); TET2/DNMT3A/IDH1-2/ASXL1/EZH2 epigenetic lesions; del(5q) with RPS14 haploinsufficiency, loss of miR-145/146a, and raised TIRAP; TP53 with a complex karyotype; and the marrow microenvironment (CXCL12, SCF, TPO…<br>[🗺️ Map](../../myelodysplastic-syndrome/mds_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../myelodysplastic-syndrome/mds_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../myelodysplastic-syndrome/mds_references.md) · [📄 README](../../myelodysplastic-syndrome/README.md) |

| 269 | Cardiovascular | [**Myocarditis**<br><sub>MYO</sub>](../../myocarditis/) | <a href="../../myocarditis/myo_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../myocarditis/myo_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="myo"></a> | Viral (CVB3, SARS-CoV-2, adenovirus, HHV-6) infection of cardiomyocytes via CAR/ACE2 receptor entry, then viral replication and activation of TLR3/7/9, RIG-I, MDA5, and cGAS-STING innate immunity, then NF-kB, IRF3/7, and the NLRP3 inflammasome, then an IFN-alpha/beta/gamma, TNF-alpha, IL-1beta, and IL-6 cytokine storm, then NK cells and M1 macrophages…<br>[🗺️ Map](../../myocarditis/myo_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../myocarditis/myo_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../myocarditis/myo_references.md) · [📄 README](../../myocarditis/README.md) |
| 270 | Ophthalmology | [**Progressive Myopia**<br><sub>MYP</sub>](../../myopia-progression/) | <a href="../../myopia-progression/myp_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../myopia-progression/myp_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="myp"></a> | Computing refraction not as a state variable but as the difference between three optical components — axial length, cornea, and lens — shows by calculation that every myopia control treatment acts on axial length alone while the refractive endpoint is biased by lenticular compensation.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../myopia-progression/myp_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../myopia-progression/myp_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../myopia-progression/myp_references.md) · [📄 README](../../myopia-progression/README.md) |
| 271 | Musculoskeletal & Neuromuscular | [**Myotonic Dystrophy Type 1**<br><sub>DM1</sub>](../../myotonic-dystrophy/) | <a href="../../myotonic-dystrophy/dm1_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../myotonic-dystrophy/dm1_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="dm1"></a> | CTG trinucleotide repeat expansion in DMPK (over 50 to 10,000+) creates nuclear CUGn RNA hairpins and nuclear RNA foci, sequestering MBNL1 (loss of function) while activating PKCbetaII to hyperphosphorylate CUGBP1/CELF1 (gain of function), reverting splicing to the fetal programme.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../myotonic-dystrophy/dm1_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../myotonic-dystrophy/dm1_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../myotonic-dystrophy/dm1_references.md) · [📄 README](../../myotonic-dystrophy/README.md) |
| 272 | GI & Hepatobiliary | [**NAFLD/MASLD**<br><sub>Metabolic-Associated Steatotic Liver Disease</sub>](../../nafld-masld/) | <a href="../../nafld-masld/nafld_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../nafld-masld/nafld_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="nafld"></a> | Insulin resistance causes excessive hepatic lipid accumulation (steatosis), then oxidative and ER stress, then Kupffer cell activation and the NLRP3 inflammasome, then hepatocyte death (apoptosis, ballooning), then hepatic stellate cell (HSC) activation, then TGF-beta1/SMAD2/3, LOXL2, and MMP/TIMP, then fibrosis, cirrhosis, and HCC.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../nafld-masld/nafld_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../nafld-masld/nafld_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../nafld-masld/nafld_references.md) · [📄 README](../../nafld-masld/README.md) |

Resmetirom (THR-beta) and GLP-1 agonists.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../nafld-nash/nafld_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../nafld-nash/nafld_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../nafld-nash/nafld_references.md) · [📄 README](../../nafld-nash/README.md) |
| 274 | Neurology | [**Narcolepsy Type 1**<br><sub>NT1</sub>](../../narcolepsy/) | <a href="../../narcolepsy/narc_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../narcolepsy/narc_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="narc"></a> | HLA-DQB1*06:02 susceptibility plus H1N1/Pandemrix molecular mimicry leads CD4+/CD8+ T cells and NK cells to destroy lateral hypothalamic orexin neurons autoimmunely (about 70,000 cells, 85-95% lost), so CSF hypocretin-1 falls below 110 pg/mL and OX1R/OX2R signalling is lost to the LC (NE), TMN (histamine), VTA (DA), and DRN (5-HT)…<br>[🗺️ Map](../../narcolepsy/narc_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../narcolepsy/narc_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../narcolepsy/narc_references.md) · [📄 README](../../narcolepsy/README.md) |
| 275 | Neonatal & Paediatric | [**Necrotising Enterocolitis**<br><sub>NEC</sub>](../../necrotizing-enterocolitis/) | <a href="../../necrotizing-enterocolitis/nec_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../necrotizing-enterocolitis/nec_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="nec"></a> | Written as a closed positive feedback loop hanging on one thing — enterocyte integrity — the model shows by calculation that there is a load at which NEC becomes possible and a separate load at which it becomes inevitable, and that between the two it is an invasive episode, not the load, that decides the outcome.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../necrotizing-enterocolitis/nec_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../necrotizing-enterocolitis/nec_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../necrotizing-enterocolitis/nec_references.md) · [📄 README](../../necrotizing-enterocolitis/README.md) |
| 276 | Neonatal & Paediatric | [**Neonatal Hyperbilirubinaemia**<br><sub>NHB</sub>](../../neonatal-hyperbilirubinemia/) | <a href="../../neonatal-hyperbilirubinemia/nhb_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../neonatal-hyperbilirubinemia/nhb_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="nhb"></a> | Links the total bilirubin that is measured to the free bilirubin that actually injures the brain through the saturable albumin binding isotherm, and decomposes phototherapy into two reactions: reversible photoisomerisation and irreversible lumirubin formation.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../neonatal-hyperbilirubinemia/nhb_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../neonatal-hyperbilirubinemia/nhb_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../neonatal-hyperbilirubinemia/nhb_references.md) · [📄 README](../../neonatal-hyperbilirubinemia/README.md) |
| 277 | Neonatal & Paediatric | [**Neonatal Opioid Withdrawal Syndrome**<br><sub>NOWS</sub>](../../neonatal-opioid-withdrawal-syndrome/) | <a href="../../neonatal-opioid-withdrawal-syndrome/nows_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../neonatal-opioid-withdrawal-syndrome/nows_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="nows"></a> | Writing withdrawal not as a concentration but as the difference between the neuroadaptive set point written in utero and the current inhibitory tone on GIRK derives together why the maternal dose changes only the time of onset rather than the severity, and why the weaning rule alone accounts for the entire threefold spread in treatment duration.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../neonatal-opioid-withdrawal-syndrome/nows_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../neonatal-opioid-withdrawal-syndrome/nows_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../neonatal-opioid-withdrawal-syndrome/nows_references.md) · [📄 README](../../neonatal-opioid-withdrawal-syndrome/README.md) |
| 278 | Oncology | [**High-Risk Neuroblastoma**<br><sub>NB</sub>](../../neuroblastoma/) | <a href="../../neuroblastoma/nb_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../neuroblastoma/nb_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="nb"></a> | Separating anti-GD2 antibody efficacy from its dose-limiting toxicity into two Fc arms with different Hill coefficients, and placing them in three target compartments whose permeability differs 200-fold, so that its behaviour as a therapy for marrow minimal residual disease and the futility of dose escalation both follow arithmetically.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../neuroblastoma/nb_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../neuroblastoma/nb_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../neuroblastoma/nb_references.md) · [📄 README](../../neuroblastoma/README.md) |

Requires 80% or more SERT occupancy, against 60% in depression. 5-HT1A autoreceptor desensitisation accounts for the 2-4 week delay in response.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../obsessive-compulsive-disorder/ocd_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../obsessive-compulsive-disorder/ocd_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../obsessive-compulsive-disorder/ocd_references.md) · [📄 README](../../obsessive-compulsive-disorder/README.md) |

CPAP, weight loss, and adjunctive drug therapy.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../obstructive-sleep-apnea/osa_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../obstructive-sleep-apnea/osa_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../obstructive-sleep-apnea/osa_references.md) · [📄 README](../../obstructive-sleep-apnea/README.md) |

| 320 | Endocrine & Metabolic | [**Pompe Disease**<br><sub>GAA Deficiency</sub>](../../pompe-disease/) | <a href="../../pompe-disease/pompe_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../pompe-disease/pompe_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="pompe"></a> | Autosomal recessive GAA mutation (17q25.3) leaves 30% or less residual acid alpha-glucosidase activity, so lysosomal alpha-1,4/alpha-1,6 glycogen accumulates, forming giant LAMP1+ lysosomes, blocking autophagy (LC3-II, p62), and leaking glycogen into the cytoplasm, with Z-line streaming, reduced mitochondrial complex I/IV activity, and satellite cell depletion in skeletal muscle, myocardium, and diaphragm…<br>[🗺️ Map](../../pompe-disease/pompe_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../pompe-disease/pompe_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../pompe-disease/pompe_references.md) · [📄 README](../../pompe-disease/README.md) |
| 321 | Psychiatry & Neuropsychiatry | [**Post-Traumatic Stress Disorder**<br><sub>PTSD</sub>](../../post-traumatic-stress-disorder/) | <a href="../../post-traumatic-stress-disorder/ptsd_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../post-traumatic-stress-disorder/ptsd_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="ptsd"></a> | A chronic neuropsychiatric disorder following exposure to life-threatening trauma, beginning with noradrenaline- and glucocorticoid-mediated memory consolidation in the amygdala (BLA) fixing an excessively strong fear memory (Cahill and McGaugh 1998 Trends Neurosci).<br>[🗺️ Map](../../post-traumatic-stress-disorder/ptsd_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../post-traumatic-stress-disorder/ptsd_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../post-traumatic-stress-disorder/ptsd_references.md) · [📄 README](../../post-traumatic-stress-disorder/README.md) |
| 322 | Neurology | [**Postherpetic Neuralgia**<br><sub>VZV Reactivation</sub>](../../postherpetic-neuralgia/) | <a href="../../postherpetic-neuralgia/phn_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../postherpetic-neuralgia/phn_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="phn"></a> | Chronic neuropathic pain (NRS 3 or more) persisting for 90 days or more in the dermatome after herpes zoster: reactivation of latent VZV in the DRG or cranial ganglia (ORF63, IE62) causes ganglionitis, secondary Wallerian degeneration, IENF density below 50% (PGP9.5 punch biopsy), and Schwann cell demyelination, so peripheral noci…<br>[🗺️ Map](../../postherpetic-neuralgia/phn_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../postherpetic-neuralgia/phn_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../postherpetic-neuralgia/phn_references.md) · [📄 README](../../postherpetic-neuralgia/README.md) |
| 323 | Psychiatry & Neuropsychiatry | [**Postpartum Depression**<br><sub>PPD</sub>](../../postpartum-depression/) | <a href="../../postpartum-depression/ppd_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../postpartum-depression/ppd_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="ppd"></a> | Built around the transient trough in tonic inhibition created by the product of allopregnanolone, which collapses within hours of delivery, and the delta-GABA_A receptor pool, which recovers over weeks; covers brexanolone, zuranolone, sertraline, and sleep protection together.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../postpartum-depression/ppd_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../postpartum-depression/ppd_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../postpartum-depression/ppd_references.md) · [📄 README](../../postpartum-depression/README.md) |
| 324 | Gynaecology & Reproductive | [**Primary Postpartum Haemorrhage**<br><sub>PPH</sub>](../../postpartum-hemorrhage/) | <a href="../../postpartum-hemorrhage/pph_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../postpartum-hemorrhage/pph_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="pph"></a> | Writing postpartum haemorrhage not as a coagulation disease but as a flow problem against a uterine blood flow of 750 mL/min, and taking placental bed patency as the fourth power of uterine tone, derives both the treatment ladder and the knife-edge between life and death from a single contractility parameter.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../postpartum-hemorrhage/pph_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../postpartum-hemorrhage/pph_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../postpartum-hemorrhage/pph_references.md) · [📄 README](../../postpartum-hemorrhage/README.md) |
| 325 | Neurology | [**Postural Orthostatic Tachycardia Syndrome**<br><sub>POTS</sub>](../../postural-orthostatic-tachycardia-syndrome/) | <a href="../../postural-orthostatic-tachycardia-syndrome/pots_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../postural-orthostatic-tachycardia-syndrome/pots_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="pots"></a> | A chronic orthostatic intolerance syndrome in which the baroreflex response to the 500-1000 mL of venous blood shifting into the leg and splanchnic capacitance vessels on standing is inadequate, or the heart rate response excessive; criteria follow the Sheldon 2015 Heart Rhythm Society consensus (adults dHR 30 bpm or more, adolescents 40 or more, within 10 minutes of standing or tilt, without orthostatic hypotension)…<br>[🗺️ Map](../../postural-orthostatic-tachycardia-syndrome/pots_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../postural-orthostatic-tachycardia-syndrome/pots_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../postural-orthostatic-tachycardia-syndrome/pots_references.md) · [📄 README](../../postural-orthostatic-tachycardia-syndrome/README.md) |
| 326 | Rare & Genetic | [**Prader-Willi Syndrome**<br><sub>PWS</sub>](../../prader-willi-syndrome/) | <a href="../../prader-willi-syndrome/pws_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../prader-willi-syndrome/pws_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="pws"></a> | Writing the loss of paternal expression at 15q11-q13 as a single scalar — prohormone convertase PC1/3 activity — and giving each of five branches exactly one dimensionless escape ratio derives algebraically which branch fails first and which measurement is blind in principle, describing satiety as a harmonic mean and food seeking as a bistable state.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../prader-willi-syndrome/pws_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../prader-willi-syndrome/pws_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../prader-willi-syndrome/pws_references.md) · [📄 README](../../prader-willi-syndrome/README.md) |
| 327 | Gynaecology & Reproductive | [**Preeclampsia**<br><sub>Eclampsia</sub>](../../preeclampsia/) | <a href="../../preeclampsia/pe_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../preeclampsia/pe_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="pe"></a> | Incomplete extravillous trophoblast (EVT) invasion leaves the spiral arteries incompletely remodelled, causing placental ischaemia and hypoxia, then HIF-1alpha, then increased sFlt-1 secretion, which sequesters VEGF and PlGF (sFlt-1/PlGF ratio above 38) alongside raised sEng, so VEGFR2 signalling and eNOS activity fall, with falling NO and rising ET-1 and ROS causing endothelial dysfunction, raised SVR, and hypertension (SBP 140 mmHg or above)…<br>[🗺️ Map](../../preeclampsia/pe_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../preeclampsia/pe_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../preeclampsia/pe_references.md) · [📄 README](../../preeclampsia/README.md) |
| 328 | Psychiatry & Neuropsychiatry | [**Premenstrual Dysphoric Disorder**<br><sub>PMDD</sub>](../../premenstrual-dysphoric-disorder/) | <a href="../../premenstrual-dysphoric-disorder/pmdd_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../premenstrual-dysphoric-disorder/pmdd_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="pmdd"></a> | Starting from the observation that luteal phase hormone concentrations are the same as in controls, the model writes neurosteroid-GABA-A transmission as an inverted-U non-monotonic function and describes the disorder as a gain defect in which receptor composition senses the rate of change.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../premenstrual-dysphoric-disorder/pmdd_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../premenstrual-dysphoric-disorder/pmdd_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../premenstrual-dysphoric-disorder/pmdd_references.md) · [📄 README](../../premenstrual-dysphoric-disorder/README.md) |
| 329 | Gynaecology & Reproductive | [**Preterm Labor**<br><sub>sPTB</sub>](../../preterm-labor/) | <a href="../../preterm-labor/ptl_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../preterm-labor/ptl_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="ptl"></a> | The placental CRH clock, functional progesterone withdrawal, intra-amniotic infection, decidual thrombosis, and uterine overdistension converge on three final common pathways — myometrial contraction, cervical ripening, and membrane weakening — and because tocolytics inhibit only the contraction pathway, a multiplicative delivery hazard shows they can buy only 48 hours.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../preterm-labor/ptl_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../preterm-labor/ptl_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../preterm-labor/ptl_references.md) · [📄 README](../../preterm-labor/README.md) |
| 330 | Endocrine & Metabolic | [**Primary Aldosteronism**<br><sub>PA</sub>](../../primary-aldosteronism/) | <a href="../../primary-aldosteronism/pa_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../primary-aldosteronism/pa_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="pa"></a> | Conn syndrome: autonomous adrenal cortical aldosterone hypersecretion (APA, BAH) suppressing renin, raising the ARR, retaining Na+, wasting K+, and causing metabolic alkalosis.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../primary-aldosteronism/pa_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../primary-aldosteronism/pa_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../primary-aldosteronism/pa_references.md) · [📄 README](../../primary-aldosteronism/README.md) |

UDCA and obeticholic acid.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../primary-biliary-cholangitis/pbc_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../primary-biliary-cholangitis/pbc_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../primary-biliary-cholangitis/pbc_references.md) · [📄 README](../../primary-biliary-cholangitis/README.md) |
| 332 | Renal & Urologic | [**Primary Hyperoxaluria**<br><sub>PH</sub>](../../primary-hyperoxaluria/) | <a href="../../primary-hyperoxaluria/ph1_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../primary-hyperoxaluria/ph1_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="ph1"></a> | Because oxalate is a terminal metabolite, PH is not a severity but an unclosed mass balance, and urinary and plasma oxalate, eGFR, ESKD, and systemic oxalosis are all generated from a single quantity: residual AGT activity.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../primary-hyperoxaluria/ph1_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../primary-hyperoxaluria/ph1_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../primary-hyperoxaluria/ph1_references.md) · [📄 README](../../primary-hyperoxaluria/README.md) |

Cinacalcet and parathyroidectomy.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../primary-hyperparathyroidism/phpt_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../primary-hyperparathyroidism/phpt_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../primary-hyperparathyroidism/phpt_references.md) · [📄 README](../../primary-hyperparathyroidism/README.md) |

| 336 | Endocrine & Metabolic | [**Prolactinoma**<br><sub>PRL</sub>](../../prolactinoma/) | <a href="../../prolactinoma/prl_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../prolactinoma/prl_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="prl"></a> | Reduced D2 receptor density in a lactotroph tumour releases dopaminergic inhibition, so prolactin is hypersecreted, suppressing the gonadal axis and producing tumour mass effect; a dopamine agonist suppresses both secretion and growth.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../prolactinoma/prl_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../prolactinoma/prl_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../prolactinoma/prl_references.md) · [📄 README](../../prolactinoma/README.md) |

| 409 | Infectious Disease | [**Visceral Leishmaniasis**<br><sub>VL</sub>](../../visceral-leishmaniasis/) | <a href="../../visceral-leishmaniasis/vl_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../visceral-leishmaniasis/vl_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="vl"></a> | Starting from the fact that drug and parasite never meet in plasma, the model writes every killing term in terms of intramacrophage concentration and amphotericin nephrotoxicity in terms of the free plasma concentration of the same dose, showing by calculation that liposomal formulation moves the two integrals in opposite directions.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../visceral-leishmaniasis/vl_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../visceral-leishmaniasis/vl_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../visceral-leishmaniasis/vl_references.md) · [📄 README](../../visceral-leishmaniasis/README.md) |
| 410 | Dermatology | [**Vitiligo**<br><sub>VIT</sub>](../../vitiligo/) | <a href="../../vitiligo/vit_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../vitiligo/vit_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="vit"></a> | Melanocyte destruction on the CD8 T cell and IFN-gamma-CXCL10 axis. Repigmentation with JAK inhibitors (ruxolitinib).<br>[🗺️ Map](../../vitiligo/vit_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../vitiligo/vit_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../vitiligo/vit_references.md) · [📄 README](../../vitiligo/README.md) |
| 411 | Haematology | [**Von Willebrand Disease**<br><sub>Type 1 partial quantitative deficiency(~75%)</sub>](../../von-willebrand-disease/) | <a href="../../von-willebrand-disease/vwd_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../von-willebrand-disease/vwd_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="vwd"></a> | The commonest inherited bleeding disorder, caused by quantitative deficiency of the VWF gene (12p13.31) — type 1 partial deficiency about 75%, type 3 complete deficiency — or by qualitative dysfunction (type 2A defective multimerisation, type 2B GPIbalpha gain of function, type 2M defective binding, type 2N defective FVIII binding).<br>[🗺️ Map](../../von-willebrand-disease/vwd_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../von-willebrand-disease/vwd_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../von-willebrand-disease/vwd_references.md) · [📄 README](../../von-willebrand-disease/README.md) |
| 412 | Oncology | [**Waldenström's Macroglobulinemia**<br><sub>WM</sub>](../../waldenstrom-macroglobulinemia/) | <a href="../../waldenstrom-macroglobulinemia/wm_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../waldenstrom-macroglobulinemia/wm_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="wm"></a> | MYD88 L265P (about 95%) gives constitutive NF-kB activation and the CXCR4 WHIM mutation (about 35%) enhances marrow retention, driving lymphoplasmacytic proliferation and a monoclonal IgM paraprotein with hyperviscosity syndrome, cryoglobulinaemia, and anti-MAG neuropathy. Seven scenarios: BTK inhibitors (ibrutinib, zanubrutinib), rituximab, bendamustine, bortezomib, and venetoclax.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../waldenstrom-macroglobulinemia/wm_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../waldenstrom-macroglobulinemia/wm_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../waldenstrom-macroglobulinemia/wm_references.md) · [📄 README](../../waldenstrom-macroglobulinemia/README.md) |
| 413 | Rare & Genetic | [**Wilson's Disease**<br><sub>WD</sub>](../../wilsons-disease/) | <a href="../../wilsons-disease/wd_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../wilsons-disease/wd_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="wd"></a> | Loss of ATP7B function disrupts copper metabolism: mutation of ATP7B, a P-type Cu-ATPase (p.His1069Gln in 35% of Europeans, p.Arg778Leu in 20% of Asians), reduces biliary copper excretion and fails to load copper onto apoceruloplasmin, so copper accumulates in hepatocytes (above 250 ug/g dry weight), saturates MT, and raises NCBC (non-ceruloplasmin bound cop…<br>[🗺️ Map](../../wilsons-disease/wd_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../wilsons-disease/wd_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../wilsons-disease/wd_references.md) · [📄 README](../../wilsons-disease/README.md) |
| 414 | Rare & Genetic | [**X-linked Adrenoleukodystrophy**<br><sub>X-ALD</sub>](../../x-linked-adrenoleukodystrophy/) | <a href="../../x-linked-adrenoleukodystrophy/xald_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../x-linked-adrenoleukodystrophy/xald_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="xald"></a> | Models the structure in which one lesion — loss of ABCD1 — creates two independent variables, the VLCFA accumulation that is measured and the bistable cerebral inflammatory switch that is not, so that the drug which fixes the marker and the drug which fixes the disease come apart.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../x-linked-adrenoleukodystrophy/xald_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../x-linked-adrenoleukodystrophy/xald_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../x-linked-adrenoleukodystrophy/xald_references.md) · [📄 README](../../x-linked-adrenoleukodystrophy/README.md) |
| 415 | Renal & Urologic | [**X-Linked Hypophosphatemia**<br><sub>PHEX gene Xp22.11 loss-of-function (X-linked dominant, ~20-30% de novo)</sub>](../../x-linked-hypophosphatemia/) | <a href="../../x-linked-hypophosphatemia/xlh_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../x-linked-hypophosphatemia/xlh_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="xlh"></a> | The commonest inherited rickets/osteomalacia, an X-linked dominant disorder caused by loss-of-function mutation of the PHEX gene (Xp22.11), about 20-30% de novo. (ADHR arises from gain-of-function mutation of FGF23 itself, ARHR1/2 from loss of DMP1 and ENPP1 respectively, and TIO from ectopic FGF23 secretion by a mesenchymal tumour, each producing a similar phenotype.)<br>[🗺️ Map](../../x-linked-hypophosphatemia/xlh_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../x-linked-hypophosphatemia/xlh_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../x-linked-hypophosphatemia/xlh_references.md) · [📄 README](../../x-linked-hypophosphatemia/README.md) |
| 416 | GI & Hepatobiliary | [**Zollinger-Ellison Syndrome**<br><sub>ZES</sub>](../../zollinger-ellison-syndrome/) | <a href="../../zollinger-ellison-syndrome/zes_qsp_model.svg"><img src="../../zollinger-ellison-syndrome/zes_qsp_model.png" width="190" alt="zes"></a> | Writing acid output not as a state variable but as the product of parietal cell mass x active pump fraction x secretagogue stimulus derives the high-dose, short-interval requirement of ZES from the asymmetry that hypergastrinaemia raises factors 1 and 3 while every acid suppressant touches only factor 2.<br>[🗺️ Map](../../zollinger-ellison-syndrome/zes_qsp_model.svg) · [⚙️ mrgsolve](../../zollinger-ellison-syndrome/zes_mrgsolve_model.R) · [📚 Refs](../../zollinger-ellison-syndrome/zes_references.md) · [📄 README](../../zollinger-ellison-syndrome/README.md) |

---

## ⚠️ Disclaimer

Every model in this library is a **qualitative / semi-quantitative QSP model for
educational and research purposes**. The models are assembled from the published
literature and clinical trial data, but they have not been independently verified or
certified, and **must not be used directly for clinical decision-making,
prescribing, or regulatory submission**. Parameters and assumptions are
illustrative approximations; fitting and validation against real patient data would
be required separately.

## 📖 References & Tools

- QSP in R with mrgsolve: <https://vantage-research.net/qsp-in-r/>
- gPKPDviz — an mrgsolve-based Shiny tool for PK/PD simulation
  - Paper: <https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10941578/>
  - Code: <https://github.com/Genentech/gPKPDviz/>
